ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u58	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Health Committee, I have the honour to present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare, print No 1976. The bill submitted for the first time since 2007 envisages a systemic increase in expenditure on healthcare funding. The proposal provides a realistic basis for increasing financing for health protection. Proposed in the project path to the 6% indicator GDP in 2025 envisages a gradual increase in the burden on health care on the state budget, which is a rational and secure action in the area of state finances. This is also pointed out by the substantive opinion of the Law presented by the Office for Sejm Analysis, which states: The Government's draft law on the amendment of the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds contained in print no. 1976 deserves support. This is the first draft law to increase the systemic financing of health care services from public funds. The project does not involve increasing the financial burden of citizens, it does not provide for imposing more danins, but merely provides for transfers of funds. According to the assumptions of the submission over the next decade, the expenditure on health will increase by more than PLN 500 billion. The draft envisages a regulation by the Minister for Health indicating the objectives for which the additional measures foreseen for health protection will be allocated. In particular, the obligations set out in Article will be taken into account. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland concerning the provision of special health care to children, pregnant women, disabled persons and the elderly. The government bill entered the Sejm on 31 October and on 3 November was addressed to the first reading in the Health Committee. The first reading of the project took place on 8 November. There was a lively discussion over the provisions of the bill. In the course of the work, amendments were tabled, two of which were designed to correlate the draft law with the so-called law. Hospital networks have been approved by the committee. The Health Commission's report on the government draft law is in print no. 1993. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! After first reading and considering the draft law, the Health Commission asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u92	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels and certain other laws, print No 1973. The project entered the Sejm on 30 October 2017. The Commission carried out its first reading and examined the draft at its meeting on 8 November. According to the information submitted by the government party, the purpose of the draft law is to implement into the Polish legal order the provisions of Directive 2015/1513/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 9 September 2015 amending Directive 98/70/EC relating to the quality of petrol and diesel fuels and amending Directive 2009/28/EC on the promotion of the use of energy from renewable sources, as well as to make adjustments to the existing rules on the functioning of the market for biocomponents and liquid biofuels. With regard to the proposed systemic changes, the proposal introduces instruments aimed at adapting the current regulatory framework to the current situation on the fuel market. The corrective action is particularly important in the context of a significant increase in official fuel demand, which is the result of an effective reduction by the current government in the functioning of the grey zone in Poland. The purpose of the proposed amendments is therefore, inter alia, to: facilitating the achievement of the national indicative objective by reducing the real level of this obligation and thereby reducing the costs of its implementation, but at the same time ensuring the need for biocomponents at the current level. It should be borne in mind that increased consumption of diesel translates directly into the need to add more and more biocomponents to this liquid fuel. For example, in 2016 the Commission adopted a proposal for a - and let me remind you that the energy package sealing the market started to apply from 1 September 2016 - the demand for methyl esters increased by more than 17% compared to 2015 and set at a record level of over 1130 thousand. a. At the same time, it should be stressed that the declared production capacity of national biocomponent manufacturers is indicated at less than 1.2 million tons. The draft law, despite the corrections made at the level of the NCW, sets the need for biocomponents at a level similar to that reported in 2016, and thus does not lead to a deterioration of the situation of agricultural producers or processing industry. Moreover, there are no amendments proposed, e.g. the replacement fee would further increase the demand for biocomponents to a level significantly exceeding national production capacity, which would result in Poland's dependence on the purchase of biocomponents abroad. In addition, the project introduces a number of legal solutions, which include instruments aimed, firstly, at adapting the rules to the Union directives by allowing for double inclusion in the implementation of NCW biocomponents produced from raw materials set out in the Annex to the Act, an indication of the minimum share of advanced biofuels in 2020 and a restriction on food biofuels in 2020; secondly, limiting the grey zone and marketing of B100 biofuels by introducing the necessity, mandatory addition of biocomponents to liquid fuels, introducing a ban on the issue of NCW attestations for biocomponents used in NCW and introducing a register of entities implementing NCWs; thirdly, reducing the costs of NCWs for fuel companies and adapting the levels of this obligation to the capacity of the production industry by introducing reduction factors for 2018 and 2019; and a substitution fee; and fourthly, making the implementation of NCWs more flexible by changing the definition of liquid fuels so as to allow the wider addition of liquid biocarb to liquid fuels and introducing the capacity of NCW with co-hydrogenation technologies since 2020. At the meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on 8 November this year. the first reading of the draft law took place. During the meeting there was a general discussion during which representatives of industry organisations did not raise objections to the draft law. The individual provisions were then discussed. At the request of the representatives of the Legal Office of the Sejm, the representatives of the Ministry of Energy provided additional explanations on the provisions indicated by the Office in order to clarify the intentions of the applicants for the project in question. In addition, at the request of the Legislative Office of the Sejm, editorial and legislative changes were made in order to clarify the provisions in the amended laws and to give them the correct wording from the point of view of the principles of correct legislation. No substantive changes were made at the committee meeting. The Commission for Energy and Treasury adopted a draft law amending the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels and certain other laws, taking into account the editorial and legislative amendments proposed by the Legislative Office of the Sejm. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u93	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask Mr Dariusz Kubiak to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u94	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Act amending the Law on Liquid Biocomponents and biofuels and certain other laws amends the laws on environmental protection, freedom of business, on the system of monitoring and control of fuel quality, as well as on the amendment of the Act on the system of monitoring and control of fuel quality and certain other laws. The current law on biocomponents and liquid biofuels regulates the functioning of the market for biocomponents and liquid biofuels in Poland. Rules cited above. the laws implement into the national legal system the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the promotion of the use of energy from renewable sources. These relate primarily to the achievement at European Union level of the 10% renewable energy target for transport in 2020 , as well as the fulfilment of sustainability criteria by bio-components used to implement the above mentioned. Target. The calculation of biocomponents towards the achievement of the objectives set out in Directive 2009/28/EC and the possibility for biocomponents to qualify for public support schemes are strictly dependent on the fulfilment of sustainability criteria by biocomponents. These criteria include, inter alia, the required minimum reduction of greenhouse gas emissions achieved by biocomponents in relation to fossil fuels and the identification of sites from which raw materials for the production of biocomponents should not be obtained. The most important changes made by Directive 2015/1513, are linked to the restriction of the use of biocomponents produced from cereals and other high-starch plants, sugar and oil crops and crops grown primarily for energy purposes on agricultural land as main crops and to the increase in the use of so-called "maize' crops. advanced biofuels. The issue of calculating emissions resulting from indirect land-use change is also an important element of Directive 2015/1513. It is desirable to achieve a high level of consumption of advanced biofuels in the European Union by 2020. Each Member State should aim to ensure that the share of renewable energy in all modes of transport is at least 10% in 2020 final energy consumption in transport in that Member State. If Member States' plans to achieve their national objectives are available, they should be published in order to increase market transparency and predictability. The Directive also extends reporting obligations to Member States, and this amendment aims to increase transparency and to improve the supervision of certification systems carried out by the European Commission. In addition, the amendment aims to bring national legislation into line with the current situation on the market for biocomponents and liquid biofuels in Poland. These are in particular changes aimed at adapting or clarifying the rules, optimising existing reporting obligations or facilitating the fulfilment of obligations imposed on obliged entities. The proposed law is compatible with European Union law. Nor is the proposed regulation expected to have a significant impact on the micro, small and medium-sized enterprises sector. The Act does not introduce new additional obligations for such undertakings. The Act will enter into force on 1 January 2018. The exception is the provision of an article on the possibility to include co-hydrogenation products in the NCW, which shall enter into force on 1 January 2020. Mr. Marshal, I would also like to convey on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the Law and Justice of the amendment to this bill. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u95	Thank you very much. Please take the floor of Mr Paweł Bańkowski.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u96	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the report of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels and on certain other laws, prints No 1991 and 1973. The draft law entered the Sejm on 30 October 2017 and was addressed to work in the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, where the first reading took place on 8 November. The main objective of the amendment is to implement Directive 2015/1513 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 9 September 2015 amending Directive 98/70 relating to the quality of petrol and diesel fuels and amending Directive 2009/28 on the promotion of the use of energy from renewable sources. The obligation to implement the Directive expires in September 2017. The draft in particular regulates the permissible participation of biocomponents produced from food raw materials and the principles of admissibility and applicability of the so-called. advanced biofuels and bio-components enabling them to be twice included in the national indicative target obligation. The most important changes introduced by the Directive concern the restriction of the use of biocomponents produced from cereals and other high-starch crops, sugar and oil crops and crops grown primarily for energy purposes on agricultural land as main crops and the increase in the use of so-called crops. advanced biofuels, as well as the calculation of emissions resulting from indirect land-use change. The most important objectives for the implementation of the Directive include reducing the costs of achieving the national indicative target borne by fuel manufacturers and importers, introducing solutions to achieve the national indicative target primarily based on the potential and technologies available on the market, and improving the transparency of this market, especially for those obliged to achieve the national indicative target. Finally, the proposals adopted in the draft law cover the following issues: the definition in the provisions of the Act until 2020 of national indicative targets instead of the determination of those levels under the Council of Ministers Regulation, together with the leaving of delegations to issue them in subsequent years, the definition in the provisions of the Law of values of reduction coefficients instead of the determination of those levels under the Regulation of the Minister of Energy, together with the leaving of delegations to determine them in subsequent years, the introduction of a substitute fee, i.e. an instrument, according to which in the years 2018 to 2019 the implementation of the national indicative target at the level of mines. 85% the level of the entity's performance in a given calendar year exempts from the obligation to designate a penalty for the remaining unfulfilled part of the national indicative target instead of the obligation to pay a substitute fee, the setting of limits in the provisions of the Act for biocomponents produced from raw materials which entitle the dual calculation of biocomponents to the national indicative target by 2019 and the clarification of the issues related to the level of penalties imposed by the President of the Energy Regulatory Office for the failure to meet the national indicative target. It should be pointed out that the changes in this respect significantly reduce the very size of the penalty, which was a call for entrepreneurs obliged to achieve the national indicative objective. The draft law also provides for changes aimed at simplifying the production of biofuels by farmers for their own use. Such changes have been made to enable farmers to carry out the above mentioned changes. activities without having to have a tax warehouse. The draft law also includes the obligation to inform the President of the Energy Regulatory Office annually of the activities which have led the entity to be subject to the obligation to pursue its national indicative objective. The scope of the amendment of this project includes the Law of 25 August 2006 on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels, the Law of 27 April 2001. Environmental Law, the Law of 2 July 2004 on Business Freedom, the Law of 25 August 2006 on a system for monitoring and controlling the quality of fuels, and the Act of 11 July 2014 amending the Act on a system for monitoring and controlling the quality of fuels and certain laws. High Izbo! The justification for the draft amendment shows that the minimum share of biocomponents produced from raw materials set out in Part A of Annex IX to the Directive has been reduced from 0,5% up to 0,1% due to the fact that currently in Poland there is a shortage of biocomponents based on innovative technologies and there is limited raw material potential for plants based on existing installations. In view of the above, the question arises: Does the Ministry of Energy intend to take action - and if so, which - in order to achieve the target reference rate of the share of biocomponents produced from raw materials set out in the Directive at a minimum level of 0,5% ? Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u98	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I wanted to present the club's position regarding the government draft law amending the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels and some other laws. The draft law takes into account the new provisions of the European Union: Directives on the quality of petrol and diesel fuels and on the promotion of the use of energy from renewable sources. Changes in the functioning of the biocomponents and liquid biofuels market are proposed, including in the way the national indicative target is achieved. The national indicative target is the minimum share of other renewable fuels and biocomponents contained in liquid fuels or liquid biofuels used in all modes of transport in the total quantity of liquid fuels and liquid biofuels consumed during the calendar year in road and rail transport, calculated at calorific value. An entity pursuing the national indicative target shall ensure that, in a given calendar year, at least the minimum share of other renewable fuels or biocomponents contained in liquid fuels or liquid biofuels used in all modes of transport, whether by any legal or actual activity, or used by it for its own use in the total quantity of liquid fuels and liquid biofuels used in road and rail transport, disposed of by any legal or actual activity, or used by it during a calendar year for its own use. In this case, the Minister for Energy is to determine by regulation the minimum level of implementation of the NCW, taking into account the state of technical knowledge in this respect resulting from the research of these fuels or their experience in their use. The Act is important for fuel companies, it affects the agri-food sector and also the processing sector. Here the questions arise: What are the possibilities for production of biocomponents? What are the plans of the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Ministry of Energy to increase the use of biofuels? The increase in biocomponent production is generally a risk of rising food prices and should also be addressed. Agricultural land intended for the cultivation of crops for consumption purposes is intended for energy purposes. The increase in the area of crops under energy crops, i.e. rapeseed, affects the situation on the cereals market. If we take into account the climate problems and, consequently, the decline in yields, we can predict or even fear an increase in the prices of other cereals. Legislation must not lead to the transformation of nature only and exclusively into the production of plants intended for energy purposes. And here we have to balance these proportions very carefully. The draft law reduces the real level of NCW, assumes the introduction of reduction factors and a substitute fee. The increase in recorded fuel sales offsets the NCW reductions. And here we must remind ourselves of the obligation for biocomponents to comply with the criterion of 50% reduction of greenhouse gas emissions of NCW. until 1 January 2018 Does the bill take account of the increased demand for biocomponents? Will the adoption of this law mean higher costs in the oil sector? Because it is also an important issue for the final recipients. Are we expected to increase the amount of biocomponents imported? How much are we able to provide biocomponents on the Polish market ourselves? To what extent it is intended to increase the use of second generation biofuels, i.e. biofuels that are produced from agricultural waste or wood waste? The Act provides for the possibility of implementing the NCW through co-hydrogenation by national fuel producers. Consequently, what amount of effort will be needed to prepare such production in terms of logistics or technology? The development of biofuel technology must go along with the trend, of course also created by legislation, to reduce CO2 emissions. These alternative fuels should also support end-users with a view to forcing CO2 emissions reductions. Kukiz.15 is very supportive of the use of biofuels, but it is still necessary to approach crops in Poland very rationally so that it does not affect food prices. Thank you very much.

The far-reaching discrepancy between the decision-making centers in the European Union's climate and environmental policy and the current capacity of Polish mining and energy groups to implement it is worrying. Therefore, it is necessary to develop and adopt Poland's energy strategy without undue delay by the government. The Free and Solidarnia Circle states that only the formulation of a national energy strategy based on the doctrine of synergy of energy sources produced in the so-called. carbon-nuclear-ozoic triads with a share of 1/3 in the Polish energy mix by each of them, while supporting the development of distributed energy, can start changes to ensure the competitiveness of the Polish reborn industry. Waiting for the outcome of negotiations with the European Union in the context of the proposed provisions of the Winter Package and the associated games of interest of strong pressure groups leads to delays that cannot be made back. The global trends and climate policy of the European Union are well known, and should therefore, taking into account the coastal environment, mobilise the full potential of competence resources, so that an efficient organisational structure can be established that can take control of the energy transformation process of Poland. The primary task is therefore to create an anticipatory strategic cycle, which will build a critical mass of good change in Polish energy. In this context, the achievement of the European Union's objective of achieving a 10% share renewable energy in transport is of marginal importance by 2020, as the revolution of electromobility sets new horizons. The experience of more advanced countries in implementing electromobility projects shows that it is not necessary to occupy the best land for energy crops, such as rapeseed, for the production of biocomponents, as the development of advanced technologies makes it possible to produce cheap electricity in cycles of carbon-nuclear and hydro-nuclear processes. Returning to the law itself: the change in the national indicator target - NCW - is justified in relation to the current situation on the fuel market after the entry into force of the so-called "new" target. the fuel package. In the light of the explanations made by Minister Andrzej Piotrowski at the meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, it appears that a reasonable compromise has been made between the interests of bio-component manufacturers and the interests of consumers directly charged with the cost of production of bio-components. The introduction of new mechanisms, such as mandatory blending, a new definition of liquid fuels taking into account the superstandard addition of biocarbons, a substitute fee and reducing factors for the years 2018 to 2019, as well as the possibility of achieving the national indicative target from 2020 through co-hydrogenation of fuels will facilitate the achievement of the target. By the way, it should be noted that the need for a real reduction in the NCW is also due to an effective reduction in the operation of the grey zone in the fuel market, resulting in a more than 20% increase in the sales of fuels on the market. The mechanisms introduced by the law will also foster the fight against tax fraud. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle strongly supports the government bill amending the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels, which creates a new framework for the fuel market. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u115	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Commission on Energy and the Treasury on the government draft law amending the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels. The draft amendment of the law was prepared due to the need to implement the provisions of EU directives in the Polish legal order. The European Union, which was initially very favourable to the production of biofuels from edible plants, has changed its position in recent years, arguing that, due to the limited resources of arable land, food-oriented cultivation should be a priority. The development of the biofuel industry has become an extremely important factor in the development of agriculture, which has a positive impact on the fight against monocultures, but also contributes to the cost-effectiveness of agricultural production. According to data provided by Minister Andrzej Piotrowski during the work of the committee, in Poland more than 70,000. agricultural holdings are engaged in the production of rape. The current rape harvest, which amounted to around 3 million t, will mostly be used for the production of biocomponents. According to the Ministry, despite the reduction in the National Indicator Objective, the demand for national biocomponents will be maintained at the current level. Thus, the Act guarantees a surplus of approximately 2.3 million t of rape for agricultural producers and at least 300,000. t corn and other cereals. The main argument put forward by the Minister at the committee meeting to justify the need to reduce the level of the National Indexing Objective was the increase in official fuel consumption, which led to a consumption of 17% between 2015 and 2016. more biocomponents. This was due to a specific economic miracle, the increase in the official fuel market reached more than 20%. This was achieved through the implementation of the fuel package, i.e. the introduction of normality in the market, which was taken over by the fuel mafia. The PO-PSL coalition was deaf to the calls of honest-working entrepreneurs, who already in 2012 alarmed the rulers about the introduction of illegal fuel into the Polish market. Unfortunately, their voice remained unheard. Also farmers are currently not hearing requests for a draft law containing a national protein index. This project was prepared by Kukiz'15 already in January this year. Unfortunately, the Ministry of Agriculture remains deaf to farmers' appeals. As previously the PO-PSL coalition has contributed to stealing the state budget from VAT, now the Minister of Agriculture of the Government of the PIS allows the bankruptment of Polish farms, agreeing to the import of genetically modified soy pellets from the USA, Argentina, Brazil. In summary, it should be said that the 2006 Biocomponents and Liquid Fuels Act has served well for the Polish farmer and the economy, while fulfilling the EU Directive and stabilising farmers' incomes. The current amendment of this law should not reduce farmers' incomes. However, the EU sees a threat from the competitiveness of biocomponents and food production. There will be further reductions in the use of plants for biocomponents, therefore the regulations proposed in the project Kukiz'15 concerning the participation of Polish protein sources in feedingstuffs should be introduced immediately and support for American, Argentine and Brazilian farmers should be discontinued. The Kukiz'15 Club welcomes this project. In the context of recent events and the proposal tabled by the PIS Members, I would add that the JOWs must remain.

In the course of the work of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy, substantive amendments were adopted, inter alia. aimed at extending the catalogue of persons who may establish social cooperatives. This change would lead to the full purpose of social cooperatives, namely social and professional reintegration of people at risk of social exclusion. At the same time, this draft law regulates the provisions concerning the categories of persons who may benefit from the Labour Fund for the establishment or accession of a social cooperative. The most important changes proposed in the draft concern, among others. strengthening the role of workers of social cooperatives who are not members of social cooperatives, including through their inclusion in social, professional and decision-making and consultation processes in social cooperatives. An important change in existing legislation is also intended to facilitate the establishment of social cooperatives by conditional reduction of the number of founders from five to three people. This three-member cooperative must reach a standard number of five members within 12 months. The draft amendment to the Social Cooperatives Act also provides for the introduction of a new support instrument. This includes measures to create a job as well as financing the cost of remuneration. According to existing legislation, people with disabilities could receive funding from PFRON to contribute to social cooperatives. Currently, after the changes, we add another to the existing form of support. This concerns the possibility of applying for PFRON measures to start an economic activity within a social cooperative, as well as the creation of jobs in a social cooperative for unemployed, jobseekers who are both disabled. The amendment also strengthens social and professional reintegration. The proposed provisions also provide for the possibility of reimbursement of social security contributions and part of the employer's costs paid for employees of non-member social cooperatives and at the same time those at risk of social exclusion. Another element is the working practices in social cooperatives for participants in occupational therapy workshops. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! All ww. and other arrangements contained in the Act amending the Social Cooperatives Act will facilitate the establishment and functioning of social cooperatives. High Izbo! This provision is intended to remind social cooperatives that they should adapt their statutes to the amended law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u138	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the social security system and some other laws, print No 1974, while the report is contained in print No 2004. At first, I'd like to say a few words about the bill. This bill actually contains one, but very important change. In 1999 a limit was introduced for the payment of pension and pension contributions of 30 times the average wage and above that basis. If someone makes more than 10 700 for this year, approximately, two social security contributions are no longer charged: pensions and pensions. For other insurances, including social security, such as accident insurance, sickness insurance, these contributions shall be paid out. However, as I said, since 1999, all the wealthiest Poles, those who earn the most, and this is about 2% all insured persons did not have their pension and pension contributions paid. This applies to the basis, not to the form of employment. All insurances that exceeded 30 times in the year had no contributions paid. This applies both to job interviews and to all others for which pension and pension contributions are paid. The bill, which I am talking about, abolishes this limit and after the adoption of the law all Poles will pay contributions from the full basis, depending on how much they earn. The first reading of this draft law took place at the meeting of the Sejm on 9 November. The project was addressed to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy. Yesterday, a committee meeting took place, which had examined the proposal, adopted it, and voted on the proposals submitted. E.g. a request was made for a public hearing which was not supported. Before proceeding, representatives of all trade unions, workers' unions, but also employers' unions, were given the floor. Representatives of local governments and the Supervisory Board of ZUS also spoke. It was, I will not hide, critical voices, but unfortunately it was often due to a misunderstanding of the project. [[Misunderstanding?]] Yeah, out of misconception. Madam, you will probably have the floor and you will speak. Let me tell you what happened during the committee work. If the representative of "Solidarity" was talking about hurting workers with the abolition of this limit, and he was talking about miners, I believe that this is entirely due to the misunderstanding, because this law is very beneficial for such people. It is very good that miners get a good wage for their hard work, but if we apply the limit and pension contributions are not paid on this salary, then later these miners will have a limited pension... [[Either way, they're gonna be limited.]] ...and it will be difficult for them to move from a large pensionable age to a low retirement pension. Therefore, I believe that the introduction of this limit in 1999 by the 1998 laws was very unfair and harmful to those who pay contributions. It was unfair, because why someone who makes very little money, these contributions pay off, has such a duty, and someone who makes very much does so only up to a certain amount and then has an unpaid salary. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u182	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To begin with, I may actually repeat this justification, which I presented to the High House and at the first reading of the bill, and during yesterday's committee meeting. [[And we know it.]] I would like to say that the aim of the project is to extend the principle which is actually applicable in the social security system, also in other segments of the social security system, the principle that all income derived from activities which are covered by the social security obligation is subject to a premium obligation. This rule shall apply without exception to health insurance. All the income that we make in the context of the employment relationship, on other grounds, is covered by a 90% premium on health insurance. We don't apply a 30-fold limit here. Persons who exceed the threshold of 30 times the average monthly salary shall pay health insurance contributions on the total revenue generated. Let me remind you that the same rule applies to sickness insurance. I don't want to develop this thread. The same rule applies to accident insurance. All income generated under the employment relationship shall be covered by a contribution to the Labour Fund. [[For years.]] The total income generated under the employment relationship shall be covered by the contribution to the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund. I think we can say, in this context, that the principle is that all the income we earn from the business covered by the insurance obligation is subject to a premium obligation. With regard to this thread, which Mr Cymanski mentioned, namely that this principle is applicable in pension and pension insurance, contrary to appearances, it only applies to one category of insured persons - people with income of less than 30 times the average monthly salary. [[Discrimination.]] All insured, 98% insured persons whose pension and pension contributions are less than 30 times the average monthly salary per year shall have contributions paid... The amount of the contribution is based on all their income. [[100%.]] So I think these are issues that clearly confirm that the principle in the security system is that all income is covered by a social security contribution. The designer's intention is to apply the same principle to pension and pension insurance. It is also an opportunity to address a few far too far-reaching questions that have emerged in the discussion, commenting on them or answering some questions. The first issue concerns the possibility for the Social Insurance Institution to implement this law. I know perfectly well that the Supervisory Board of the Social Insurance Institution is the body of the Social Insurance Institution which is legally authorised to issue opinions on draft normative acts. I myself am the Chairman of the Supervisory Board of the Social Insurance Institution. The discussion on this subject took place yesterday, despite what you have raised in the course of the discussion, the premise that the supervisory board followed in adopting a resolution in this regard was not the question of whether it was possible or impossible to implement, implement this project or this law amending the Social Security Act. The body of the Social Insurance Institution which is responsible for operational activities in the field of social security shall be the Management Board of the Social Insurance Institution. I can say that President Uścińska has repeatedly assured that the Social Insurance Institution is ready to implement this law from 1 January 2018. They grow by organizing the labour market. The stability of the Social Insurance Fund is guaranteed by an orderly and stable labour market. This type of action has already been taken in 2016 and is taking place in 2017. I think that such a factor, which very well reflects the situation, the financial condition of the Social Insurance Fund, is the so-called. the rate of the Social Insurance Fund's expenditure on contributions. This indicator is at a record high level of over 79%. This is an indicator that has not been recorded in recent years. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u196	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the Corporate Income Tax Act. The draft submitted allows the Minister of Development and Finance to provide the public with information containing tax data from tax returns submitted by corporate tax payers. The proposed regulation will cover the largest taxpayers whose revenues in the previous year will exceed the equivalent amount of EUR 50 million, as well as taxpayers acting in the form of tax groups - regardless of the amount of revenue generated. Why 50 million euros? This is, of course, a reference to the Law on business freedom and the definition of a large trader whose annual net turnover from the sale of goods, products and services and financial operations exceeds the amount in PLN corresponding to the equivalent of EUR 50 million. According to the tax returns held by the Ministry of Finance, the chosen group of taxable persons is liable for 61% of the tax payable by all taxable persons settling corporation tax. The proposed solution will therefore cover only those taxpayers whose impact on the economy and the state budget is important both from the point of view of their position on the market and because of their potential impact on the revenue of the State budget. Conditions relating to the above mentioned condition: the level of revenue currently meets around 2 000. taxpayers. In addition, there are about 60 tax groups of capital. So you can say cards on the table. The public disclosure of the tax data of the largest CIT taxpayers will contribute to the real pressure of the public on taxpayers to properly comply with their tax obligations. We cannot accept a situation where income taxes are paid by small and medium-sized companies, and large, particularly international corporations are using tax avoidance schemes. Every citizen in Poland will have the opportunity to see how much the largest companies operating in Poland actually pay to the state budget, compare it. The proposed instrument is a response to the global problem of tax avoidance and erosion of the tax base of the countries where the income is generated. This is, of course, an international problem, discussed among others. by the G20, the OECD or the European Union. The scale of this problem and its negative impact on the functioning of individual countries justify reaching for measures that have not yet been applied. In this context, it is justified that, in particular, in the area of CIT, globalisation, the development of the digital economy, technological development, freedom of movement of capital, liberalisation of trade and similar factors change in a diametric manner the state-of-the-art relationship between the state and taxpayers operating on the global market. Further leaks of Paradise Papers, Panama Papers and Luxleaks clearly confirm that the biggest taxpayers use several hundred aggressive tax optimization schemes to escape their obligations to societies and hide untaxed profits in tax havens. This process has been characterised by unprecedented dynamics in recent years, which is why the response is international. Its manifestation is the conclusion of the BEPS project by the OECD, as well as the rapid decision of Poland to ratify the MLI Convention. Another example is the so-called. the ATAD Directive laying down provisions to combat tax avoidance practices which have a direct impact on the functioning of the internal market. The European Union is currently working on financial reporting, which will increase the availability of tax information and help implement a strategy for a fair and effective corporate tax system in the European Union. I will say to our Seym opposition that the European Commission is calling "enhanced public control', "social control' or'rebuilding citizens' confidence in the justice of national tax systems'. The solution proposed by us is also not unusual in Europe, and it is already a complete nonsense to claim that it violates the principle of the presumption of honesty of companies. The mere fact that a taxable person appears on this list will mean, no less, no more, only that he fulfils the criterion of income and not that e.g. wrongly cleared his income tax liability. It can therefore be said that the draft law is an equation to the highest Scandinavian standards in force, e.g. in Denmark, where information on costs, turnover, income or taxes of individual companies is available on the publicly accessible website of the register of Danish companies. A similar solution also exists in Sweden, where it is possible to obtain information about the activities carried out by entrepreneurs at the request of the interested party. The project envisages that the following tax data will be made public: the name and VAT of the taxable person, tax year, revenue, income/loss costs, tax base and finally tax alone. This information may be extended by an indication of the percentage of the tax amount in the taxable person's gross profit shown in the financial statements for the tax year to which the disclosure relates. In this case, making such information publicly available will be of a general nature, covering all taxable persons achieving a certain level of income whose data are made public, of course outside tax capital groups for which the gross profit category shown in the report is inadequate. According to the project, the public disclosure of tax data will apply to taxable persons whose value of income exceeded the amount in PLN corresponding to the equivalent of EUR 50 million as well as to taxable persons with the tax status of the group, regardless of the level of income achieved by that group. We want the relevant tax data to be published by the end of September of the year following the end of the tax year of the taxpayer concerned. At the same time, in order to enable the information to be made available to the public to be correctly established, it is assumed that it will contain data as at the first day of the month preceding the month in which the information is to be made public. Also in order to determine the taxable persons whose data will be disclosed to the public, the data resulting from the submitted tax returns as at the first day of the month preceding the month in which the information is to be made public will be used. The introduction of such a solution takes into account the possibility for taxable persons to revise their declarations and it is therefore necessary to specify a strict date by which the data provided for public disclosure will be determined. The provisions also provide for the possibility for the taxable person to submit a request for deletion or rectification of data, which will include inter alia. its justification. If such an application is not taken into account, the competent minister shall issue an appropriate decision if it would result in non-compliance with the facts. The new regulations will apply from the tax year which started before 1 January 2018 and will end after 31 December 2017. It will also make public the data of taxable persons arising from the testimonies for earlier tax years from the tax year beginning after 31 December 2011. That period shall be linked to the prescribed provisions of the Tax Order and the limitation period for tax obligations. High Izbo! According to the report Runaway Taxes the corporation tax in Poland is only 5.33% total tax revenue, while the average for OECD countries is almost twice as high. That's not much more than 1%. GDP of Poland. This is the worst result among the countries surveyed. Some experts, including the European Commission, cite data that refer to the amount of the CIT gap comparable to the VAT gap. Whether we are talking about a few or tens of billions of PLN per year of budget impact, it is about the giant money that the Polish state loses. It's also an element of unfair competition. Tax savings obtained by large international holdings are used to fight competitively with Polish entrepreneurs. Tax savings allow for the use of dumped prices. In this way, the non-tax holding company also eliminates competitors on the market. This is how this mechanism works, and the public has the right to know who in Poland pays taxes and who avoids them. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Public Finance Commission, after considering the above draft law at its meeting on 15 November 2017, asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u218	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On 15 November this year. the first reading of the government draft law on amending the Law on Guarantees and Guarantees granted by the State Treasury and certain legal persons and certain other laws, contained in print No 1905, took place. Today we are working on a committee report. At the committee meeting, the first reading took place, i.e. a general discussion was held, and then the committee began a detailed examination of the project. The draft law aims to provide a sustainable and continuous source of support for small and medium-sized enterprises and micro-enterprises by facilitating and ensuring continuous access to guarantees and guarantees provided by the Treasury and certain legal entities. The way to achieve this is to create a National Guarantee Fund at the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. In this sense, it is the implementation of the Responsible Development Strategy, a government strategy to which the government has joined, which implements this strategy step by step. In addition, the proposed solution will relieve the State budget of the expenditure and costs resulting from the guarantees and guarantees provided. This is also an important objective of this project. Experience has shown that guarantees and guarantees are a very effective way of stimulating entrepreneurs' lending, they are an effective way of mobilising free resources, investments, and therefore they are a way of making companies more invested with all these positive consequences, for example for the labour market, for economic activity, for improving the income situation. Guarantees covering part of the risk, even up to 60% the borrowing, allow for much higher amounts of credit, up to 40% more than the guarantees given. So far, Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego has provided guarantees and guarantees of around 9 billion per year since 2013, and this programme has been renewed annually by the relevant regulations of the Minister of Finance. In addition, in the previous arrangements, the guarantee and guarantee measures came almost entirely from the state budget and were a burden on the state budget. In the proposed solution, instead of the state budget, we are introducing other sources of resources for the creation of this fund, the National Guarantee Fund. They will come from among others. from Union funds cleared. They will also come from funds relocated by BGK, as well as voluntary participation of interested parties. In this situation, the state budget can only play a complementary role in the event of a clear need to support this national fund and is therefore not the main source of funding. The solutions adopted are expected as much as possible because of the economic situation and because of the facilitation of Polish entrepreneurs' access to capital, which will have positive effects also in the field of production and, above all, investment, which we all care about and what we all expect. After the discussion, the project was considered... Two amendments were tabled... comments from the Legislative Bureau, followed by two parliamentary amendments. The first one concerned Article. 4, i.e. the entry into force of the Act. According to the draft law, the Act enters into force on 1 January 2018, but at the same time the guarantees and guarantees provided by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego in connection with the implementation of government programmes from 1 January to 30 June 2018 will apply to existing provisions. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u220	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position on the government draft law amending the Law on Guarantees and Guarantees given by the State Treasury and certain legal persons and certain other laws. The proposed amendment, in print No. 1965 and 2006, aims to create a legal framework for the implementation of the two strategic projects foreseen in the Responsible Development Strategy. In the area: capital for development and small and medium-sized enterprises is envisaged to introduce a sustainable system-based instrument to support the development of small and medium-sized enterprises using a loan guarantee based on the National Guarantee Fund. Currently, the de minimis guarantee scheme, which has been in operation since 2013, which provides the small and medium-sized enterprises with access to credit guarantees, is financed predominantly from the State budget. It was designed as an ad hoc anti-crisis tool. The proposed amendment proposes that, instead of a programme renewed annually by government decisions, a system providing sustainable, stable access to preferential guarantees should be introduced to businesses. The volume of guarantees granted should remain close to that of previous years - about PLN 9 billion. Instead of budget support for the overwhelming amount of the expenditure and costs of the guarantees provided as State aid or de minimis aid, an instrument would be set up to create the financial assembly of Union funds from the financial instruments of the EU programmes of the current and past financial perspective of the European Union and the funds reallocated from the capital of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego and any voluntary participation of stakeholders. The State budget would serve as a supporting role as a guarantor of the system. This instrument would be the National Guarantee Fund created by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. The expected result of the project is the continuation of the de minimis guarantee scheme with a reduction in the involvement of the state budget, improved access to finance, increased competitiveness of the small and medium-sized enterprises sector, reduced risk of lending to the small and medium-sized enterprises and thus created incentives for banks to increase lending. According to the Minister of Finance, the change in the provision of guarantees will save the amount of PLN 1.6 billion in the state budget for 2018 and 2028. The proposed rules should have a positive impact on the labour market in the micro, small and medium-sized enterprises sector. Between 2013 and 2016, the de minimis guarantees created or maintained around 100,000. jobs. The benefits set out here for our citizens, small and medium-sized enterprises and the state budget resulting from this project mean that the Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of the amendment presented by the government. Thank you very much.

PLN 900 million, from European funds from the current and past financial perspective of the Union, from commission fees for guarantees granted by the Fund, from interest on the interest on the funds of the National Guarantee Fund, as well as any voluntary participation of stakeholders, as well as, of course, from contributions from the state budget, which will ensure the liquidity of the fund and guarantee the functioning of the entire system. Powering the National Guarantee Fund from non-budget sources should, of course, bring savings and reduce the involvement of the state budget in the guarantee and guarantee process for small and medium-sized enterprises. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u244	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the draft Law on Printing No. 1964. The draft law amending the Act on National Tax Administration and the Law The provisions introducing the Act on National Tax Administration contain provisions aimed at implementing optimal solutions for the provision of IT services not only to the organizational units of the National Tax Administration, but also to all entities of the Ministry of Finance. It takes into account the implementation of the Responsible Development Plan and the need to consolidate the services and IT resources of the finance department in order to improve their quality. The current budgetary unit of the Ministry of Finance - IT Centre of the Finance Resort has planned to take over the tasks of providing IT services for the entire area of the finance department. IT staff and IT resources of tax administrations and National Tax Information will be transferred to the unit. The proposed change will allow for the centralisation of resources and workers and, above all, for the introduction of uniform management processes, better use of employees' competences and increased security of centralised systems. Purchasing costs and the number of procedures will be reduced, system maintenance costs will be reduced. This unit's position will change with respect to the initially planned National Tax Administration Information Technology Centre by excluding it from the structures of the National Tax Administration and transferring it to the direct supervision of the Minister responsible for the relevant departments of government administration. The Financial Resort IT Centre will operate on the basis of the assets, personnel and competence of the currently functioning state budget entity supervised by the Minister responsible for public finance, established on the basis of the provisions of the Public Finance Act of 27 August 2009, which is statutoryly responsible for providing IT services to the IT infrastructure finance department. The Financial Resort IT Centre will take over IT tasks from the tax administration and National Tax Information Offices. This will extend the area of operation of the Financial Resort IT Centre, which, in addition to the organizational units of the KAS, will also serve the organizational cells of the Ministry of Finance, including those which do not form part of the structure of the KAS. This means that in one organizational unit they will be implemented, among others. processes related to the planning and development of IT infrastructure for the entire finance department, the preparation and implementation of new local and regional IT systems, as well as the support processes of IT service users - excluding KAS investigative IT, coordinated by the Centre for Inquiry located in the Opole Customs and Skarbowy Office. In conclusion, the bill submitted allows for the full optimization of IT solutions for the entire finance department, which is why the Law and Justice Club supports the bill submitted and will vote in favour of it. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u252	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on National Tax Administration and the Law introducing the Act on National Tax Administration, print No. 1964 and 2007. The first reading and analysis of this project were carried out at the meeting of the Public Finance Committee. As is apparent from the title, this project concerns two laws: the Act on KAS and the Law Provisions introducing the Act on KAS. The main solution to this project is the creation and strengthening of the Financial Resort IT Centre directly subordinate to the finance minister. The new centre will be created by linking the IT cell in the Ministry with the National Tax Administration IT Centre currently under the responsibility of the Head of the National Tax Administration. The government assumes that processes related to the planning and development of IT infrastructure for the entire finance department, the preparation and implementation of new local and regional IT systems and the management of IT service users will be implemented in this single organisational unit. This means, according to the government, improving the operation of IT services and systems, which will be an important step towards increasing tax collection. It is the quality of information systems that is largely dependent today on the effective fight against tax crimes and thus reducing the deficit of the state budget. According to the project's assumptions, on 1 January 2019 employees from 16 tax administrations and the National Tax Administration will be transferred to the Financial Resort IT Centre, those who are performing computer science tasks today, so I do not suspect that this is another staff bill releasing everyone, throwing them out on the street and assuming that they are taking their own. I don't have a basis for that. The Centre of Information Technology of the Financial Resort will also take over the assets and tasks relating to IT from tax houses and National Tax Information, excluding, of course, investigative IT, because these tasks are coordinated and will be coordinated by the Technical Centre of Inquiry Information which is currently located in the Opole Customs and Skarbowy Office. The Centre for Informatics of the Finance Resort is intended to ensure the development and maintenance of the central IT systems of the Ministry, mainly in areas such as the state budget and the budget of local government units as well as public debt. The solution proposed by the government means centralising the IT resources that, according to the government, will become safer, and the use of them will be more rational. The consequences of this solution are also the introduction of uniform management processes and better use of the skills of IT specialists. The decision-making process is to proceed faster and more efficiently. The IT consolidation will also result in lower purchasing costs and lower IT infrastructure maintenance costs. It seems so obvious. We judge in accordance with the government's opinion that this modern and efficient IT organization will respond well to the needs of the economy and therefore the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u271	The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u272	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I would like to present a report by the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending laws to prevent the use of the financial sector for tax fraud, print No 1880. The main objective of this draft law is to reduce the VAT gap caused by fraud most often carried out by organised criminal groups. These groups generate artificial transactions, so-called. carousel transactions. Bank accounts in these transactions are used for multiple, rapid transfers of the same cash in order to fake business. The real purpose of the VAT carousel is to extort VAT, which causes billions of dollars of losses to the State Treasury or to all of us - taxpayers. The project assumes automated and electronic access by the Head of National Tax Administration to some banking information. This will allow for an immediate analysis of this information and a rapid reaction of the tax authorities. According to the project, the head of the National Tax Administration will carry out in his IT system an analysis of the risk of using banks and SKOKs for tax fraud. The clearing house will play an important role in the risk analysis system. The project assumes that, based on the results of the analysis, the head of KAS will be able to block the account of the eligible entity for 72 hours - in settlements - with the possibility of extending the blockade for up to 3 months. The draft provides for electronic lists of entities which have not been registered as taxable persons for VAT purposes. The preventive impact of the introduced system of risk analysis and the ability of tax authorities to react quickly should be: a significant reduction in tax fraud, an improvement in the business conditions for all taxpayers by restoring fair competition on the market and a strengthening of legal certainty as a result of the publication of information on registered VAT taxpayers. The first reading of this draft law took place on 11 October this year, and then the project was addressed to the Committee on Public Finance, which authorised the Permanent Sub-Committee to monitor the tax system for further legislative work. The subcommittee examined the draft law at three meetings on 26 October and 6 and 14 November of this year. Amendments were tabled during the subcommittee meetings with representatives of non-governmental institutions, which were unanimously adopted by the subcommittee. The government draft law, together with the amendments, was adopted by the subcommittee and the report was again addressed to the Committee on Public Finances with a request for its adoption. At yesterday's meeting of the Public Finance Committee, the project was considered. Five amendments were tabled during the process, which were adopted by the committee after the discussion. The draft, together with the amendments, was adopted by the committee in a vote with no votes against. Therefore, I have the honour to recommend, on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, the adoption by the High Chamber of Government of a draft law from print No 1880. Thank you very much.

Let me just remind you that this law gives the head of the KAS the opportunity to set up a 72-hour ban on bank accounts, with the possibility of extending this period to 3 months, that in addition here we are dealing with breach of banking secrecy, commercial secrecy, generally with the oppressive state, the PiS state in which everyone acts as a suspect and any such suspect can become. It is true that an official error in this case can simply lead to the bankruptcy of the company with a light hand, because, Primo, which was not mentioned here, the occurrence of such a blockade in the company's account usually immediately results in the termination of the credit agreement of such an entrepreneur by the cooperating banks - and it is not only in the bank where such a blockade would occur, but, I suppose, in all banks - secondly, immediately each of the contractors of such an undertaking immediately terminates the agreement on the buyer loan, etc., etc. And this company, without being able to dispose of its funds collected on the account for a longer period of time, simply becomes insolvent from day to day. Half-poor, if we're dealing with a tax criminal who's going to be proven guilty. It's worse if there's such a mistake as a clerk's. The Minister assures us here that today the system has such opportunities that the likelihood of guilt in such a case is very high, but he did not answer my question whether a mistake is impossible, because we all know that there is a possibility of error. But, ladies and gentlemen, entrepreneurs and spouses of entrepreneurs are not the only people affected by the restriction of this law - which is even more incomprehensible - they are also employees of these entrepreneurs, because, as standard under enforcement titles, bank enforcement titles, where the account is blocked due to unpaid debts, employees have the right to receive a full remuneration from such an account and the bank is entitled to pay on the basis of the wage list submitted, but it is assumed that, in this law, it will be possible to carry out orders such as the sending of wages on the payroll, income tax advances and social contributions to such employees, but, ladies and gentlemen, only to the minimum wage for the work. What does that mean? This means nothing other than that if an employee of such a company, e.g. He's making 5,000. zł, he won't get the money, he'll get the minimum wage, which is 2,000. minus the charges due, which is about minus 30%. [[It's called collective responsibility.]] And that is the exact attraction of the employees of such a company to collective responsibility. And let's be honest: at this point in time, employees of such enterprises are also victims of crime, even if there is a crime, and they also fall into the rights of creditors of an entrepreneur such as the State, and in connection with the signed employment contract and in connection with the funds due to them at the end of the month also have the right to claim their claims. Worse still, let's say that for 3 months there is a blockage of such an account, the employee does not get his full salary, he has at that time to meet the obligation for e.g. the credit agreement and after 3 months there is a time in which the bank terminates the agreement for an apartment loan. And now there's an official mistake, and not only is the entrepreneur in trouble, but also his employees have far-reaching personal problems that result in them becoming insolvent, unbelievable in the system and unable to function. This is a complete element for us. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Unacceptable. I therefore submit an amendment and support for this law subject to the adoption of this amendment. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u283	Thank you very much, Member. Thank you very much. Please, Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u284	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending laws to prevent the use of the financial sector for tax fraud, i.e. 1880 and 2022 prints. The draft law was examined in the Permanent Sub-Committee for monitoring the tax system as well as in the Committee on Public Finances. Many legislative and editorial amendments have been introduced in the course of the work, as well as several substantive amendments. In general, the project aims to reduce tax fraud with the use of the financial sector, including banks and cooperative savings and credit banks. Currently, I think we are all aware that the importance of electronic transactions is increasing. These transactions were also in the area of interest, of course, in criminal groups, especially those of an organised nature. This will increase the role and importance of the analysis of financial flows and will be one of the basic procedures for identifying threats to the banking system. Electronic transactions may also use banking systems even to legitimise criminal activities that are actually designed to extort tax. An important element introduced by the bill under discussion is the ICT system, so-called. STIR, through which the clearing house will analyse the risks of using banks or SKOKs to commit crimes and tax offences related to VAT fraud. The IT system - STIR, as well as the Central Register of Invoices, which is to apply from the new year, as provided by the Ministry, should allow, it seems, to control financial flows and allow the National Tax Administration to intervene quickly, which is very important in this case - VAT fraud is a matter of rapid action. The whole system for efficiency requires close cooperation between the clearing house and banks and SKOKs, as well as the use of the data contained in the Central Register of Entities of the National Taxpayers' Register. In order to prevent criminal offences, e.g. so-called. the disappearing taxpayer, it is important to take rapid action. Therefore, the project provides for a blocking of the account at the request of the head of the KAS, as well as the refusal to register and to remove the entity from office as a VAT taxable person without having to notify that entity. So these are some difficulties for honest entrepreneurs as well. The amendments notified and adopted in the course of the work of the Permanent Sub-Committee and the Committee on Public Finance improve the exchange of information between the banking system and the National Tax Administration, clarify the technical arrangements for the exchange of this information and the effectiveness of the operation. Some of the amendments introduced are regulated e.g. issues related to legally protected information, the system's relationship with the Bank Guarantee Fund, as well as details of deadlines for the mutual transmission of information. In general, it must be stressed that the project is extensive and difficult. Theoretically, it gives us the opportunity to prosecute criminals using the banking system, but we must also be aware here of the reality that the project will impede the functioning and limit the freedom of business. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u285	Thank you very much, Member. Please, Mr Ireneusz Gains, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u286	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending laws to prevent the use of the financial sector for tax fraud, print No 1880. The main objective of this draft law is to prevent VAT fraud and, above all, the creation of tax carousels and VAT fraud in electronic transactions. During the last two term of office of the Sejm, the state budget as a result of tax fraud was robbed by tax criminals of over PLN 200 billion. This has now been effectively reduced, but on a smaller scale it continues. We are constantly losing the fruits of work and entrepreneurship of millions of Poles. The solutions contained in the draft law are intended to ensure an increase in the revenue of the state budget. The project contains two specific arrangements to secure the funds that should be used to pay the tax due before they are transferred outside the Polish banking system. The first is to block a bank account at the request of the head of the KAS, who will be entitled to block a suspected company account for 72 hours, with the possibility of extending up to 3 months. During this time, the NSA will have time to collect materials that will enable the funds to be secured against the taxes due. The second solution is the refusal of registration and the removal of the entity as a VAT taxable person, without the need to notify that entity, where the tax administration obtains information that is likely to carry out activities with a view to using the financial sector for tax fraud purposes. The current solutions do not constitute an effective barrier to tax offences, among others. because of the small amount of information available to banks to identify risks. Given the above and the ease and speed of use of IT tools, as well as the skills and organisational capacity of criminals in this area, it is necessary to take decisive action to identify and eliminate the identified risks. A tool that allows an effective solution to the above. the problems are to be primarily the IT system of the clearing house, so-called. STIR. The scheme aims to limit the operation of tax carousels based to a large extent on the use of a functioning banking system by assessing the risk of a company using banking infrastructure for tax fraud. The project also aims to extend the scope of the tasks of the National Tax Administration to the possibility of identifying, detecting and combating crimes related to the so-called. money laundering. The amendment also requires the National Tax Administration to inform law enforcement authorities of suspected crimes and to provide them with a preliminary analysis of the evidence justifying this suspicion. The proposed law will contribute to a more effective defence of the fiscal interests of the state, so that tax money will be better protected against fraud, so that it can be used, among others. for the implementation of social programmes and the modernisation of the state. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle strongly supports the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
