The disrespectful or even disdainful attitude of judges to those involved in the proceedings must be eliminated, which is often pointed out by professional plenipotentiaries. The painful experiences of citizens who seek not only formal justice in court but also a real understanding of their problems have made judicial reform necessary to restore a sense of social justice. It aims not only to eliminate pathology and eliminate the sense of harm, but also to restore respect and a high assessment of justice and thus the general public belief that Poland is a just state. The President of the Republic of Poland warned that the statutory arrangements for the reform of an institution as important to the State as the Supreme Court should not only be properly recognised and accurately diagnose problems, but also serve to rebuild justice in the State, must be socially understandable, carefully prepared and be in accordance with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. By refusing to sign the July Act, the President declared that he had prepared a draft law on the Supreme Court. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill submitted is based on an in-depth diagnosis of the actual human, competence, organizational and functional problems of the Supreme Court. The main assumptions that accompanied the creation of this project are, above all, to increase the legal protection of victims of justice and to increase the role of citizens in the administration of justice. The proposed reform weighs constitutional values so that the solutions put forward are radical as far as possible in compliance with the existing Constitution. The radicalism of reform is not an end in itself. It follows from the deep conviction of the projector that it is necessary to sensitise the judge's environment to the needs and difficulties of citizens who turn to the courts in order to obtain real support in their cases, a fair settlement of cases with which they turn. Following the proposed changes, the President refers to the existing tradition in Polish legislation, that projects concerning the functioning, in particular, of the highest judicial authorities, the Supreme Court, administrative courts, in particular the Supreme Administrative Court or the National Judicial Council, should come from the presidential office. This is a consequence of the recognition that it is the President of the Republic of Poland who has a constitutionally assigned role of arbitration in matters of the state, and such is certainly the way judges are appointed and the functioning of the highest judicial authorities. This competence of the President must not be questioned in any way. Compared to the current law, the proposal essentially introduces four groups of new solutions. The first group concerns corrective justice. The second is to improve the effectiveness of disciplinary proceedings against judges and other legal professions, and to assess them for non-professionals. In addition, the project is designed to rebuild the structure of the Supreme Court due to new competences and changes in the status of Supreme Court Judge. On the other hand, the fourth group of amendments concerns interference in the staff resources of the Supreme Court. One of the main objectives of the project is the introduction of instruments that safeguard and implement the principle of social justice. Experience from the activities of certain offices of the Polish President's Office indicates that in Poland many people have been victimized by grossly unfair judgments of the courts. These judgments are often not only unfair, but also lead to violations of human rights and freedoms as expressed in the Constitution. The introduction of a new legal measure in the form of an emergency complaint will allow Poles to believe once again in justice and the ability of courts to enforce it. The introduction of this measure is the implementation of commonly submitted demands and electoral obligations of the President of the Republic of Poland. An emergency complaint may be brought against a final judgment terminating proceedings in cases where the judgment infringes the principles or freedoms and rights of the person and of the citizen referred to in the Constitution, where that judgment grossly infringes the law by misinterpretation or incorrect application, and where there is an obvious contradiction of the essential findings of the court with the content of the evidence collected. An emergency complaint will be brought before the Supreme Court only by the authorities of the State specialising in ensuring the rule of law or having to take care of freedom and human rights and of the citizen. The emergency complaint complements the gap in the current system of exceptional remedies. An emergency complaint may be based not only on an indication of a gross violation of the law but also on the contradiction of the court's findings with the evidence collected. An emergency action other than a cassation action or a cassation will also benefit from those final judgments in which the appeal has not been used before. The construction of an emergency complaint also highlights the need for courts to respect constitutional principles and constitutionally protected freedoms and human rights and citizens. The institution of the emergency complaint is not in conflict with the institution of the constitutional complaint, on the contrary, it is a certain complement to the catalogue of measures to protect freedom and human rights and the citizen. The project shall also ensure that the social factor is involved in the identification of emergency complaints. It should be noted that the aim of this institution is to restore fundamental legal order and sense of justice by eliminating from the market judgments which infringe the constitution, grossly violating the law and, of course, contrary to the content of the evidence collected. The participation of jurors in the case of emergency complaints will introduce a very important element of social control where fundamental elements of the principle of social justice could be violated. The draft law on the Supreme Court provides for the establishment of a Disciplinary Chamber of the Supreme Court. The adoption of the proposed construction will allow for a clearer structure of control of the in-house disciplinary case-law through organisational separation in the Supreme Court of the Chamber serving this purpose. This will ensure better organisation and greater efficiency in dealing with disciplinary matters. The separation of a separate chamber of the Supreme Court will increase the transparency and accessibility of this procedure to society. The result will also be an improvement in the quality of disciplinary case-law as well as the specialization of individual judges in this normative field. In democratic societies, mechanisms must be in place to control the way in which public confidence is exercised. The regulatory system governing the pursuit of these professions has its specific characteristics based on professional ethics adapted to a particular profession. The observance of ethical standards by members of the public trust professions is essential from the point of view of a democratic society in which these professions perform a particular function. The judge, the prosecutor, the attorney-in-law must have the right trust and credibility to ensure that the application of the applicable law has social support, understanding and acceptance. Without appropriate regulation aimed at efficient and transparent judicial review, compliance with ethical standards by professionals of public confidence is impossible to achieve at full level, while increasing this responsibility is undoubtedly intended to ensure a high level of enforcement. The designer also regulates the internal disciplinary mechanism for judges of the Supreme Court, whose design assumes the existence of a two-instant disciplinary tribunal, where at first instance a disciplinary court would be held in the composition of two judges of the Disciplinary Chamber and one juror, in the second instance, three judges of the Disciplinary Chamber and two jurors. Juries in WW. cases will be appointed from among the jurors of the Supreme Court elected by the Senate for a four-year term. The introduction of mixed disciplinary formations of judges and judges aims to increase public participation in disciplinary proceedings of Supreme Court judges. This participation, given the position held by the Supreme Court in the hierarchy of judicial authorities and the important tasks entrusted to it in the field of application of the law, is justified. Such regulation implements social control, improves transparency of disciplinary proceedings and increases citizens' confidence in Supreme Court judges while preserving the professional character of formations. The Act provides for a catalogue of disciplinary penalties which have been ordered according to the principle of proportionality resulting from Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Polish Constitution. The disciplinary penalties, in accordance with the draft, will be: reprimand, reprimand, a reduction in the basic salary of the judge by 5% for a period of between 6 months and 2 years, removal from the occupied office, submission of the judge from office. The proposed catalogue of disciplinary penalties gives the disciplinary court a wide range of possible responses, allowing an adequate response to the disciplinary offence of the accused. The proposal also provides for the possibility to waive disciplinary penalties in situations of minor importance. The proposed law also requires the publication of a judgment of a disciplinary court on the website of the Supreme Court. This solution is used to inform the public about disciplinary proceedings against Supreme Court judges and their final effect, which increases the transparency of disciplinary proceedings and also increases public confidence in the morale of judges. The conviction of a Supreme Court judge in disciplinary proceedings against reprimands, a reduction in the basic salary and the removal from office will automatically entail a ban on disciplinary proceedings, participation in the College of Supreme Courts and the exercise of functions. The judgment of disciplinary action in the form of the submission of a judge of his own motion shall result in the possibility of reassigning the sentenced person to the office of a judge. Changes in disciplinary proceedings also required the inclusion in the draft legislation amending the Law on the system of common courts, the Law on the system of military courts, the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance - the Commission for the pursuit of Crime against the Polish National, and the Law on the Prosecutor's Office. For the implementation of new and at the same time extremely important functions, the project separates two new chambers of the Supreme Court: the Chamber of Extraordinary Control and Public Affairs and the Disciplinar Chamber. The draft law also settles the issue of the status of Supreme Court judges. The proposed amendments aim primarily at simplifying the procedure for selecting candidates for the post of Supreme Court Judge, ensuring the impartiality of this procedure, increasing the involvement of the President of the Republic of Poland in the appointment of Supreme Court Judges, as well as ensuring the independence of judges and strengthening their impartiality in resolving pending cases. The changes also concern the age at which the judges retire. A new accelerated procedure for the waiver of immunity is introduced. The President of the Republic of Poland will be appointed to serve as the Supreme Court judge at the request of the National Court of Justice. The draft law introduces a new, so far no requirement for candidates for Supreme Court judges, which concerns the completion of the 40th year of age. It should be noted that a similar criterion applies to judges of the Supreme Administrative Court. In addition, this requirement is to ensure that the Supreme Court is filled with persons who have the right experience, including life experience, and knowledge. The draft law also introduces a new requirement relating to non-employment, lack of employment and non-cooperation with State security authorities. This is mentioned in Article. 5th National Remembrance Institute Act. The Supreme Court will only be able to judge a person who has not been legally convicted of a deliberate criminal offence prosecuted by a public prosecution or a deliberate tax offence against which a final judgment has not been given conditionally remitting criminal proceedings for the commission of such a criminal offence. Such regulations are intended to increase both the credibility and confidence of the Supreme Court judges. As regards the circumstances resulting in the termination of the service relationship, in addition to those mentioned, the acquisition of a foreign nationality by a judge is another unknown circumstance. The exception is the situation in which the judge resigned his nationality within 30 days of the date of his acquisition. An important change also applies to the age at which the Supreme Court judges will retire. According to the proposed regulation, the Supreme Court judge will in principle retire at the age of 65. The exception is a situation where the judge makes a statement of his willingness to continue to take office. It will also be necessary to provide a certificate stating that the Supreme Court judge is capable of performing the duties of judge because of his medical condition. The President of the Republic of Poland will decide on the possibility of further taking up office. In view of the different age provisions in force so far resulting in the transition of the Supreme Court judges to rest in Article. 108 provides that, within 3 months of the date of entry into force of this Act, judges who have completed 65 years of age shall be retired unless, within one month of the date of entry into force of the Act, they apply for an extension of the possibility of ruling in the Supreme Court and the President agrees to do so. The solutions adopted in relation to the transition of the judge to rest and his age are a mapping of the solutions existing in the general court. In addition, it should be noted that a woman who is a Supreme Court judge will at her own request be able to retire at the age of 60. The draft law provides for a limitation of the possibility for judges of the Supreme Court to take up additional employment. The Supreme Court judge will be able to perform a teaching, scientific and scientific position at the Polish higher education school and work related to conducting classes within the National School of Judicial and Public Prosecutor's Office and as part of training organized by legal professional authorities, unless the total number of teaching hours exceeds 210. The introduction of an hourly restriction aims at preventing additional employment from hindering judges from carrying out their duties properly. The intention of obstructing the performance of the duties of a judge is also the basis for objecting to additional employment by the first President of the Supreme Court. An important change in the context of the existing Supreme Court Act is the proposed procedure for the accelerated examination of an application for authorisation of a judge's criminal liability when he has been caught in the act of a criminal offence, strictly defined categories of offences. These crimes will include crimes, offences punishable by imprisonment, the upper limit of which is 8 years, and communication offences as defined in Article. 177 § 1 in conjunction with Article. 178 § 1 of the Penal Code and Article. 178a § 1 or 4 of the Penal Code. In a situation where a judge is shot in the act of one of the above-mentioned judges. offences where the judge is still in custody, the disciplinary court shall adopt a resolution on the application no later than 24 hours after its influence to the disciplinary court. In disciplinary proceedings before the Supreme Court and in cases of emergency complaints, judicial and jury formations will be decided. Thus, it was necessary to regulate the way in which the jury operates and the rules of operation in the Supreme Court. The project proposes that jurors be elected by the Senate from among candidates submitted by citizens directly or through social organisations. The number of jurors of the Supreme Court is to be determined by the College of the Supreme Court. The Supreme Court jury will be elected for a four-year term. The rules of their operation are similar to those of jurors in common courts. During the transitional period following the entry into force of the Act, pending the election of the jury of the Supreme Court, the duties of the jury will be performed by the jury to the District Court of Warsaw-Prague and the District Court of Warsaw. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In presenting from this point the main assumptions of the presidential draft law on the Supreme Court, I would also ask for a fair, emotionless analysis of the project and, above all, of the fact that the draft law should contribute to the legal security of citizens and the reconstruction of the authority of the judicial authorities. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u336	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! What will the final draft law on the Supreme Court look like, we do not know, because instead of a broad consultation we have informal - not to say: secret - negotiations. This role will be practically reduced if it is not liquidated and the Supreme Court will be brought into the role of the Third Court of First Instance or the Disciplinary Court. In fact, the changes in the structure of the Supreme Court are intended to introduce to the Supreme Court judges elected by the PiS. And so on. The Chamber of Extraordinary Control and Public Affairs will be set up, where the newly appointed judges will rule, not from the former Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs, where, among other things, they will rule. on the validity of elections or appeals against decisions of the National Radio and Television Council. The President will be entitled to determine the Rules of Procedure of the Supreme Court according to the project, which means that the General Assembly of Judges of the Supreme Court will be deprived of this competence. This is a clear manifestation of the dependence of the judicial authority on the executive authority. Changes are envisaged in the procedure for the election of the first President of the Supreme Court. To date, the President has appointed the first President from among the two candidates presented by the General Assembly of Supreme Court Judges. Today, the proposed amendment provides for the choice of five candidates, which is a sign of strengthening the President's position vis-à-vis the judiciary. Changes have been made to the procedure for the election of the Presidents of the Supreme Courts in charge of each Chamber. They are now appointed by the President at the request of the first President of the Supreme Court for a term of five years. Today, a three-year term is proposed only after consulting the first President of the Supreme Court. The President would therefore be free to fill the seats of the Presidents of the various Chambers. The draft redefines the powers of the General Assembly of Judges of the Supreme Court, clearly limiting these powers, thereby weakening the General Assembly of Judges of the Supreme Court. Apart from losing the possibility of adopting the Rules of Procedure of the Supreme Court, he will be deprived of the right to elect candidates as Supreme Court Judges or to elect two members of the National Judicial Council. In the event of the application and the submission of candidates for the post of Supreme Court Judge, the amended procedure does not provide for any powers of the first Supreme Court President, which is an expression of a weakening of the independence of the judiciary. The project requires the President's approval to serve as a judge at the age of 65. This right is completely arbitrary and allows for any decision which of the Judges will continue to take its stand. An extraordinary appeal was introduced, i.e. an emergency complaint which, according to the general statement, can be brought in practically any case and within a long time after the judgment under appeal has been finalized. This is a direct undermining of the principle of protection of trust in the State and the law and the right to settle a judicial case within a reasonable period of time. In today's legal situation, we have both a constitutional complaint and a cassation complaint. The project does not show that another appeal is needed. In addition, the proposal provides for the possibility for Members and Senators to bring a complaint, whose task as representatives of the legislative authority is completely different. This will be part of a continuous election campaign. Finally, the proposal allows the Minister of Justice to choose a disciplinary court at first instance, a disciplinary hearing officer and his alternates, which means that the Minister of Justice will have a court and prosecutor. The jury in the Supreme Court will be elected by the senators who will be able to file cassation complaints. Let us remember that the Supreme Court is a court of law, not a fact, [[♪ The bell ♪]] Unfortunately, therefore, experience and knowledge will be needed. These are just some of the examples. There is little time for this, because Law and Justice have not agreed to the ten-minute speeches in the debate, but we will probably be debating in the committee, since the PiS will vote and in principle reject our proposal to reject the project at first reading. [[Applause]]

Secondly, this draft law separates the existing chambers of the Supreme Court - the Civil Chamber, the Criminal Chamber and the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs - two new chambers: the Chamber of Extraordinary and Public Affairs and the Disciplinar Chamber. The first will deal primarily with the handling of emergency complaints and will take over matters of public law that have been dealt with so far by the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs. The Disciplinary Board will have a special status among the Supreme Court Chambers so that it can deal transparently with disciplinary matters against judges and other legal professions. Importantly, proceedings will be conducted involving a social factor in the form of jurors. Thirdly, the proposed provisions on the transition to retirement should no longer give rise to concerns that any staff cleansing will occur. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u538	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Speaking of your comments during the discussion and the questions, I would first like to refer to matters relating to the conditions that need to be met in order to be a Supreme Court judge. The age of 40 years is not separated from solutions that already exist in the system, because this age is included in the Law on the system of administrative courts, because the condition for being a judge of the Supreme Administrative Court is to be 40 years old. Similarly, this law requires a ten-year traineeship in some legal profession: as a judge of another court, a prosecutor, a lawyer, a solicitor, a notary, so again these are requirements that are already in force in the system for judges of the Supreme Administrative Court. From the point of view of the Supreme Court, this is an increase in these requirements, because so far there was no requirement of age, i.e. a person who was thirty-six years old had a degree of doctor-habilitated, and she could also become a judge of the Supreme Court, even though she had no experience in the justice system. As regards matters of delegation to serve as Supreme Court Judge, delegates can only be judges, they cannot be prosecutors. Prosecutors may apply for the post of Supreme Court Judge and may not carry out judicial proceedings on the basis of a delegation. On the other hand, I do not understand why the role of the Supreme Court is being reduced, because the law itself does not contain absolutely such solutions, in no way does the systemic role of the Supreme Court be reduced. As regards the definition of the Rules of Procedure of the Supreme Court, no one disputes that the President has a very strong influence on the organisation of administrative courts, including the appointment of voivodship administrative courts. With regard to issues relating to the Presidents of the Supreme Court, there is also no risk of the President's independence, because the President will only be able to appoint the President of the Supreme Court from among those candidates who will be proposed by the Chamber. There is no threat here that the President himself, without any indication from the Chamber, will appoint the President, who will direct the Supreme Court Chamber, and this is directly due to the provisions of the bill. On the other hand, as to whether or not to continue to take up a position, it is not so that it is arbitrary. First, such a person must provide a certificate that he or she is capable of continuing his or her duties due to his or her medical condition. This is the first of the conditions. Secondly, each of these cases will be dealt with individually by the President, and therefore it is still the same as for judges in the courts at the moment where the National Courts agree to continue to perform their duties. [[Member Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz: What criteria?]] This has been the case in common courts so far, that the judge, at the age of 65, could have obtained the approval of the National Courts of Justice and there were no indications in the National Courts that, under such circumstances, the National Court of Justice had to grant consent, in others, it was not entitled to grant consent. Therefore, I do not understand why you are questioning the authority of the President here, especially his role in the system resulting from the article. 126 constitutions. With regard to the emergency complaint itself: it, its design, fills a certain gap in the system of protection of rights and freedoms. Let me remind you that according to the article. 79 of the Polish Constitution is entitled to a constitutional complaint in the event of a breach of a final or final decision, the final administrative decision of human rights and freedoms and of the citizen resulting from the Constitution. However, what is the subject of an appeal by means of a constitutional complaint is not an individual decision which has led to the violation of freedom and human rights and of the citizen, but a normative act which was the basis for its issue. This means that a person who feels affected by such unjust violations of his or her freedoms and rights by judgment or administrative decision will continue to have this adverse decision, and the only thing he or she will be able to do - if his or her constitutional complaint is dealt with positively and a judgment is given declaring the non-compliance of a normative act on the basis of a constitutional decision - is to be able to resume proceedings on the basis of his or her own request, which in turn determine the provisions of the relevant procedures. The introduction of this measure, such an exceptional measure, into the system of protection of freedoms and rights, to the system of protection against injustice, has been called for, among others. Senator Staron or Mr Wojciechowski. They have repeatedly pointed out that there are many situations in which there has been a gross violation of the law, as well as violations of constitutional freedoms and rights, and that someone has no other means to enable him to obtain a decision that gives him satisfaction, giving him a sense of justice. Another issue concerns the age at which judges retire. Not more than a year ago, on 25 November, one of the ladies who represented the Association of Polish Judges in the Senate indicated that she would very much want the judges in Poland to be able to retire at the age of 55, at the age of 60 when it comes to women, because they feel so overworked that they can no longer work. The age of 65 is categorically too much for those who carry out their judicial duties, especially if they started their judicial career in regional courts. As far as the issue of decommunising the Supreme Court is concerned, indeed, as the President stressed in his meetings with club representatives, after 1989 there was decommunization of the Supreme Court, but in the 1990s such persons returned to the Supreme Court and currently there are about 15 judges who were members of the PZPR until the end. There is only one person who was a member of the PZPR, resigned from the PZPR and was an activist for the democratic opposition. That's what it looks like right now. In addition, the President, also during meetings with the clubs, pointed out to representatives who were present that there was one person who was the head of the primary party organization in court... [[No one was a secret co-worker.]] ...one person who even wrote daily reports to KC PZPR on the implementation of martial law legislation. On matters relating to the procedure itself, I would like to remind you that it is a law of a systemic nature and therefore it does not reform the civil procedure or the criminal procedure governed by separate laws in any way. This is a law that only affects the system of the Supreme Court as this judicial authority, which ensures the correctness of the ruling by common courts and military courts. I would also like to remind you that, according to the draft regulation annexed to the draft law, the number of judges who will rule in the Supreme Court is expected to increase. According to the draft regulation, there are to be 115 of them. As far as having only Polish citizenship is concerned, there are several important issues here. Firstly, the Convention on Citizenship does not apply in Poland because, as rightly indicated here, it has not been ratified, which means that it is not the law in force here. On the other hand, no one has the right to be appointed as a judge. There is no subjective right that could be removed from the Constitution, but the article already mentioned here. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 introduces the possibility of introducing different types of restrictions or conditions to be met in order to be able to exercise certain powers or to be appointed to certain offices. And here, too, in the case of judges, it will be important to have a moral node with regard to the state of which they are citizens. Since the role of a judge is very special and acts as a public authority, a judicial authority, it seems that this requirement is most appropriate, although the Sejm will decide whether it will ultimately be adopted by the law which it will adopt. I would also like to answer your question about the amendments here. I do not know what amendments you will be making, this will be seen in our next meetings - in committee meetings or in plenary at second reading. [[But Kaczyński has already made a deal with the president, they're already notified.]] Another issue concerns the Disciplinary Chamber and its specific character. This particular character is such as to stress the independence of this Chamber and also to give this independence to these judges in disciplinary proceedings not only with regard to judges of the Supreme Court but also with regard to representatives of other legal professions. As regards the issue of the transfer of judges to rest, I would stress once again that it is not that all judges will be transferred from the vending machine to the resting state, and what is connected with this, there is also no provision in this bill that would say that they will be moved immediately by law to the first President of the Supreme Court. There are no such rules, so I do not know where the state has read, on what basis they have interpreted, but the age of 65, once again I will remind you here, is the general retirement age, as well as the age applicable to judges of common courts. Therefore, at this point, there is a convergence of principles, as if on the basis of which judges in all courts will go to rest. Emergency complaint and time limits concerning the possibility of bringing an action with it. In the case of an emergency complaint, there are two groups of conditions that must be met in order to make such a complaint. The first group are infringements which justify the possibility of bringing an emergency complaint, the second group being those who will be able to lodge a complaint. Therefore, it is not that any citizen who will be disappointed because he has a decision that does not satisfy him will be able to file a complaint with the Supreme Court. That is why it is necessary to introduce this narrow group of entities responsible for the protection of the rule of law and freedoms and rights as entitled to bring emergency complaints, so that they can first check whether any of these infringements referred to in the Act actually occur and, consequently, whether the complaint can actually be brought. You are pointing out that the 5-year period is a period of uncertainty, because you will be able to benefit from an emergency complaint at any time, and somehow you do not see that the corresponding deadlines are in place for other emergency control measures, such as those related to the request to resume proceedings. Thus, with regard to an emergency complaint, such a limitation of the 5-year deadline is not fair, with regard to other emergency control measures it is perfectly acceptable and does not pose any threat to the stability of judicial case law. On questions of Mr Sosnowski, I would like to remind you once again that the President held a meeting with the parties at the President's Palace during which the parties had the opportunity to present their various proposals for solutions concerning judicial laws. With regard to matters relating to the participation of jurors, it is, in accordance with the Constitution, a contribution of the social factor to the ruling - a social factor that is intended to express a social sense of justice. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u540	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Andrzej Duda, I have the honour to present to the High House a bill amending the Act on the National Council of Courts and certain other laws. The task of the National Judicial Council shall be to uphold the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges. The increasing number of tasks put before the justice system in recent years requires increased responsibility of the Council for the proper functioning of the justice system. The independence of the courts and the independence of the judges may prove to be a cliché when the courts fail to act efficiently and gross irregularities occur in their operation. Insanity, failure to act, revealed gross errors of justice, reduces the degree of public confidence in the judiciary to no less than the conviction that there is no objectivity in judicial case law. To date, the practice of the National Judicial Council has given rise to the assessment that the work of the Council has largely focused on matters relating to the staff of judges, while other, less relevant matters concerning the functioning of the judiciary remain on the sidelines. In addition to staff matters, it is also the task of the National Judicial Council to assess the state of the judges and to ensure that the rules of professional ethics of judges are respected. The current practice of the Council shows the need for changes of an institutional and organisational nature. The bill presented changes the way in which the members of the National Judicial Council - the body responsible for the state of the judicial staff - are elected by granting citizens, alongside judges, the right to elect candidates for members of the board. The eligible entities, according to the project, will be: a group of at least 2 000 to submit a candidate for a member of the National Judicial Council. citizens and a group of at least 25 judges in an active state. The approval of such regulation will increase the democratic legitimacy of the members of the National Judicial Council appointed by judges, strengthen transparency and allow public debate on candidacy. The project requires the Marshal of the Sejm to inform the Polish Monitor about the initiation of the procedure for submitting candidates. The application may cover only one candidate, which shall not preclude the possibility for a citizen or a judge to support more than one application. The proposed regulation does not foresee the possibility of canceling the complex signature. If a judge is appointed as a member of the Board, the President of the court competent for the candidate shall draw up the relevant information covering the candidate's judicial achievements, socially significant or precedental decisions, as well as relevant information concerning the culture of the candidate's office as judge. On the other hand, if the application concerns the President of the Tribunal himself, the President of the Supreme Court will draw up the information and, in the event that the candidate is the President of the Appeal Court, the Vice-President of the Court of Justice. Correct applications of a candidate for a member of the Council of Marshals of the Sejm shall be made available to Members without delay and shall be made public. It will also be the duty of the Marshal of the Sejm to establish the model of the application and the model of the list of citizens and the list of judges who apply for a member of the Council. Models of these documents have been annexed to the bill. The draft provides for the possibility of appealing to the Supreme Court the order of the Marshal of the Sejm refusing to accept the applicant's application. The purpose of this provision is to prevent cases of distortion of the will of supporting persons and to safeguard the right to court. According to the draft final selection from the candidates submitted, the Sejm should exercise a qualified majority of 3/5 votes so that the members of the body upholding the independence of the courts and independence of the judges are elected not only by a parliamentary majority but also by other groups. In this way, the choice will be the result of an agreement between the various groups represented in Parliament. The judges on the board should have the proper legitimacy and qualifications and personal qualities in order to be able to do so. If it is not possible to conclude an agreement between representatives in the Sejm and there is no qualified majority of 15 members of the Council, the draft involves introducing an additional selection procedure in the form of a roll-call vote. I reserve that the roll-call vote is intended to pursue only the fundamental objective of ensuring that groups outside the majority of their representatives can choose from the National Judicial Council. If a different, better procedure is presented during the legislative process to complement the election of the members of the Board to ensure that this objective is achieved, it seems possible to adopt it. The representativeness of the National Judicial Council and its social acceptance are the characteristics that ensure the implementation of one of the most important human rights guaranteed constitutionally, namely the right to court, exercised by an impartial, independent, independent and fair court. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! One of the objectives of the proposed amendment to the Act on the National Judicial Council is to waive the term of office of individual members of the Board elected from among judges. Individual terms were challenged by the Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 20 June this year. [[There is no Constitutional Court. This isn't a tribunal.]] The members of the board, who are judges, will be elected for a joint term of four years. According to the draft, the joint term of office of the new members of the Council shall begin on the day following that on which... [[You're all going to be punished for this.]] ...the last member has been chosen. The proposed amendments also include the addition to the Act on the National Judicial Council provisions applicable in a situation where the mandate would need to be filled again before the end of the term of office. Amendment introduced by Article. 20 of the Act on the National Judicial Council responds to the social expectations of increasing transparency in the procedure of state bodies, including bodies upholding the independence of courts and the independence of judges. The introduction of the possibility to broadcast the deliberations of the National Judicial Council via the Internet will increase citizens' confidence in the work of this body. The reliable information about the activities of the National Judicial Council in an area so important for the proper functioning of the justice system, namely the seat of judges, is also a very important educational function. Another change aimed at increasing the transparency of the Council's work is the modification of the directives to be taken into account in determining the mode of action of the National Judicial Council. It is essential to ensure that information on the proceedings before the Council is available and that information on the candidates and the reasons why the request for appointment is made. Proposal to add art. 44a introduces an obligation for the National Council of the Court of Justice to submit requests for the appointment of specific persons to the position of a judge, together with justification, as well as information on other candidates. The President should be given an assessment of all candidates. This will make it possible to make the right decision and provide a comprehensive view of the case. The President of the Republic of Poland should be able to examine all applications submitted in order to take a decision on the appointment of the person designated by the National Council for the position of judge. Article 4 is replaced by the following: The 6th draft provides that a member of the National Judicial Council elected on the basis of the existing provisions shall remain in office until the date of the start of the joint term of office of the new members of the Board elected by the Sejm from among the judges under the new rules. [[I wonder what your promoter thinks about this.]] The proposed regulation relates to the judgment of the Court of 20 June this year in which it was found that the individual nature of the term of office of the members of the National Judicial Council elected from among the general courts is incompatible with Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 Constitutions. The Court referred to earlier case law permitting the expiry of the term of office of a member of the Council to be indicated by law, which, according to the Court of Justice, constitutes the admissibility of a derogation from the 4-year term of office. In the view of the Constitutional Tribunal, since the term of office of Members and Senators - members of the Council is not currently individual, the term of office of the other elected members of the Council should not be so. [[There's no tribunal now, Minister, there's nothing to claim.]] It should be pointed out that the draft provides that the members of the Board elected from among the Judges shall be elected for a common term of office. The proposed regulations do not concern the term of office of other members of the Council, as their term of office is linked to the term of office. The mere termination of the term of office, or termination of the term of office of a member of the Council, is not tantamount to a departure from the constitutional principle of a 4-year term of office. It would be contrary to the Constitution to indicate in the Act e.g. A three-year term. The draft respects the duration of the term of office laid down in the Constitution. There's no rule left. So it's broken. The principle of continuity of the constitutional body is also respected, as the capacity of the current members of the Council to act will cease only on the date on which the joint term of office of the newly elected members of the national council begins. Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council 6 projects are unique. It does not deny the constitutional principle of a 4-year term, it is proportionate to the intended systemic changes. The shortening of the term of office must only be seen as an exception, but it is acceptable where there is no real possibility of introducing transitional arrangements when the rules for representation of judges in the Council are changing. The different dates for starting and ending the term of office of the members of the Board cause that changes cannot be made within a reasonable period of time. On the other hand, the consistency of the changes made and the impossibility of the functioning of the National Council of Courts according to a single concept are a value which justifies the early termination of the term of office of the elected members of the National Council. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The entry into force of the Constitution of 2 April 1997 strengthened the position of the judiciary. However, the current functioning of the judiciary requires the creation of a new organisational framework also for the National Judicial Council. The Constitution of the Republic of Poland designates a special role for the President vis-à-vis the independent judiciary in Poland. The President shall appoint judges and participate in the process of establishing the organisational structure of the judiciary. The existing discrepancies in the opinion on the draft law are due to a different perception of the role that the National Judicial Council should play. According to the President, the proposed changes will result in more effective action by the Council and will consequently improve the state of the Polish judiciary. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u557	Thank you very much. Please, Mr Ireneusz Gains, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u558	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I have the honour to present a position on draft laws amending the Act on the National Council of Courts and some other laws, i.e. the draft presented by the President of the Republic of Poland, printing No. 2002 and the Parliamentary project, printing No. 1697. According to Art. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland The National Judicial Council is the guardian of the independence of the courts and independence of judges. This is an extremely important and responsible task which should be carried out with a view to achieving the best possible functioning of the justice system. In order to achieve this objective well, it is necessary to balance and control one another by three authorities: legislative, executive and judicial. This division is clearly disturbed today. Art. 4th Constitution speaks in paragraph. 1, that the sovereignty of the Republic of Poland belongs to the nation, and in paragraph 1. 2 that the nation exercises power by its representatives or directly. Unfortunately, so far the judicial authority in Poland has chosen itself and controlled itself. The sovereign has no jurisdiction, no authority, no control. I hope that the proposed regulation on judicial reform will allow this to change. The essence of both draft laws is first and foremost to establish new rules for the selection of judges for the composition of the National Judicial Council. According to the presidential draft, the selection of judges to the National Courts of Justice is to be made by the Polish Parliament by a qualified majority of 3/5 votes. The entities entitled to submit candidates for members of the Board shall be a group of at least 2 000. citizens or a group of at least 25 judges in an active state. Where it is not possible to reach consensus on the selection of judges of the members of the Council by qualified majority, the draft provides for the introduction of a procedure of roll-call voting in accordance with the principle: one Member, one vote. However, it is already known from the information provided through the media by representatives of the President of the Republic of Poland and representatives of the parliamentary majority that the present procedure which raises doubts about its constitutionality is to be replaced by a procedure allowing opposition groups to select at least six judges of members of the National Courts Council. On the other hand, the parliamentary project provides for the choice of judges to be left to the National Courts of Justice by general, administrative and military judges. With regard to the two models presented for the selection of judges to the National Courts of Justice, we strongly opt for a presidential solution providing for the selection of judges by the Polish Sejm. I want to make it clear here: by the Sejm being a constitutional body... [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]] [[It is not for you that this applause is not for you.]] ...by the Sejm being a constitutional body of the state, not by politicians. [[Game in the O.R.]] [[And it's not for Marshal Terletsky either, so there's no doubt.]] Mr. Marshal, if you'd like to shut the courtroom down, I'd appreciate it.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u693	Thank you very much. Mrs Małgorzata Zwierzan will now speak on behalf of the Free and Solidarni wheels.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u694	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Social Policy and the Family on the citizens' bill on the restriction of trade on Sundays. The regulations contained in this proposal provide for a trade ban on Sundays, but not all. The law excludes from this prohibition specific Sundays and places where trade will be permitted. These are among others. fuel stations, pharmacies, florists and certain categories of economic operators whose operation on Christmas day may be necessary. Importantly, the bill contains provisions aimed at regulating working time on Christmas Eve and on the Holy Saturday. In the current situation, the interests of trade workers have been secured by the Labour Code and, as we all know, this level of protection is not always sufficient. It is therefore pleased that the designers have decided to include employment provisions in a separate law on Sunday. The citizens' project mentioned a trade ban every Sunday of the month. During the work on the project in the Permanent Sub-Committee on The labour market and the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy have made significant changes which limit the scope of the ban on trade only to two Sundays a month and two consecutive Sundays preceding the first day of Christmas and Sunday immediately preceding the first day of Easter. A lively discussion was created around this change. I personally believe that all Sundays should be covered by this trade ban. We shouldn't be making Sunday the usual day of the week. However, I understand the objections of the opponents to this solution and I believe that the adoption of a compromise and the adoption of two free Sundays a month is a step in the right direction, especially since it does not close the way for further amendments to this law in the longer term. I consider the tightening of sanctions to be a legitimate violation of the trade ban on Sundays. In my opinion, the transfer of legislation for breaking the ban on trade on Sunday to criminal law in a better, more effective way will affect respect for the law and reduce the scale of the ban on traders. When we examine the bill, we need to look at it from two perspectives: social and economic. According to the data presented in the explanatory memorandum and my daily observation, women are employed much more frequently in trade. It is we women who often have to reconcile work and family responsibilities. Women working on Sundays are deprived of the possibility to spend one day a week in the group closest to them. In addition, due to the time spent at work, they cannot fully participate in social, cultural life. Working on Sundays is not stressful, which is likely to result in a deterioration in your health in the long term. This is a very important issue in terms of the ageing population. All of this is reflected in studies that clearly show that those working on Sundays need a holiday day and do not consider a given day off on another day of the week or an additional salary to be sufficient compensation for their loss. I hope that the long-lost Sunday tradition will be restored as the time spent among the loved ones. This will help to strengthen relations within Polish families, and we should all care about this. We are all aware that a rested worker is a more efficient worker. As for the economic aspect, the introduction of the law will not contribute to a reduction in revenue to the state budget or to businesses. Shops will compensate themselves for fewer trading days with an increase in turnover in the days preceding the holidays. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u751	I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice a position regarding the draft resolution on commemoration of General Józef Dowbor-Musnicki on the 150th anniversary of his birth and the 80th anniversary of his death, 1954 and 1980 prints. The history of his life resembles the fate of hundreds of Polish landowners who served in armies of possessive states, and during World War I they fought for independence of Poland. Two of the general's merits are particularly important. First, the creation of the Polish Corps in Russia after the February revolution of 1917, the command of the corps and success in the fight against the Bolsheviks. Secondly, organizing nearly 100 thousandth Wielkopolska Army, commanding the Greater Poland Uprising and leading to its victory, and thus the liberation of Wielkopolska and its integration into Poland. The attitude of General Józef Dowbor-Muśnicki was characterized by patriotism and consequence also in the times of the Second Republic. As a loyalist, the general stood up against the May bombing. He patronized Lechia's patriotic academic corporations in Poznań and Batoria in Vilnius. The conservative values that he observed were uniquely confirmed by his daughters' attitudes. Janina's older daughter Lewandowska was a pilot of the Polish Army and she was the only woman to die in Katyn. In turn Agnieszka Dowbor-Musnicka's younger daughter in conspiracy worked in the Military Organization of Wolfs and was murdered by the Germans in Palmirach after the deconspiracy. In our view, the figure of General Józef Dowbor-Musnicki deserves to be reminded and remembered. In acting on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club is in favour of honoring the general by adopting a commemorative resolution on the 150th anniversary of his birthday and the 80th anniversary of his death.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-22-sejm-52-1.u798	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present a report by the Rules of Procedure Committee, the Parliamentary and Immunity Affairs Committee on the draft parliamentary bill amending the Act on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator. On 16 November 2017, the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft for first reading in the Committee on Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary Affairs and Immunity. The purpose of the draft amendment to the Act of 9 May 1996 on the implementation of the mandate of the Member and the Senator is to extend the statutory conditions which allow the rules of the Sejm, the Senate and the National Assembly to be laid down for the reduction of the salary of Members and Senators as well as for the reduction of the parliamentary diet and the loss of the right to the parliamentary diet of Members and Senators. Current wording of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 5 and Article 5 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. Article 42 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 of the Act allows the rules to be laid down for the reduction or reduction and loss of parliamentary allowances only in the cases referred to in those provisions: where a Member or a senator by gross violation of the relevant regulations prevents the work of the Sejm, the Senate or the National Assembly, as well as their bodies, and where the Member or Senator does not participate without justification in the meetings of the Sejm, the Senate or the National Assembly and their bodies. The proposal proposes that the conditions currently contained in the legislation be supplemented by the addition of a further case, i.e. infringements by a parliamentarian of the seriousness of the Sejm, the Senate or the National Assembly. The Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Affairs Committee, following its first reading and examination of the draft at its meeting on 21 November 2017, invites the High Sejm to adopt draft legislation from print no. 2008 without amendments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The government's social benefits provisions remain unchanged. The write-back to the occupational social benefit fund for teachers will be based on the base amount of 1 January 2012 and the write-down to the occupational social benefit fund for employees of public institutions will be maintained at 2013 level. The social benefit fund is frozen at the level of 2017 and the 2012 appropriations are the basis for the pension provision of uniform services. The same applies to Article. 28 projects in which the average monthly remuneration in the national economy in the second half of 2012 is assumed to be calculated. The amendment on rejection was also rejected by the PiS Members - deletion of the provisions concerning the extension of the deadline for the entry into force of the Compensation Fund set up by the Hunting Law Act of 22 June 2016. In conclusion, the project does not deserve support. On the one hand, the project proposes write-downs for people working the size from 5 years ago, and on the other hand it sanctiones increased funds for IPN, the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland and the Senate Office. The law, regardless of the boasted by the government about the state's excellent financial situation, shows that the situation of public finances is very bad. The Around Budget Act resembles a period of deep financial crisis. Without taking into account employee expectations, it allows for huge sovereign debt - I will remind you that public debt will increase by another 72 billion PLN in 2018. Members of the PSL Parliamentary Club will not vote in favour of this bill.
